Physiological and Transcriptional Responses to Saline Irrigation of Young ‘Tempranillo’ Vines Grafted Onto Different Rootstocks Ignacio Buesa 1,2,3* , Juan G. Pérez-Pérez 1 , Fernando Visconti 1,4 , Rebeka Strah 5,6 , Diego S. Intrigliolo 4 , Luis Bonet 1 , Kristina Gruden 5 , Maruša Pompe-Novak 5,7 and Jose M. de Paz 1 1 Instituto Valenciano de Investigaciones Agrarias, Centro para el Desarrollo de la Agricultura Sostenible, Unidad Asociada al CSIC “Riego en la Agricultura Mediterránea”, Valencia, Spain, 2 Ecophysiologie et Génomique Fonctionnelle de la Vigne, Institut National de la Recherche Agronomique, Institut des Sciences de la Vigne et du Vin, Villenave d’Ornon, France, 3 Edited by:         Research Group on Plant Biology Under Mediterranean Conditions, Department of Biology, University of the Balearic Tommaso Frioni,         Islands, Palma, Spain, 4 Centro de Investigaciones sobre Desertificación, Departmento de Ecología (CSIC, UV, GV), Valencia, Catholic University of the Sacred       Spain, 5 Department of Biotechnology and Systems Biology, National Institute of Biology, Ljubljana, Slovenia, 6 Jožef Stefan Heart, Italy     International Postgraduate School Ljubljana, Ljubljana, Slovenia, 7 School for Viticulture and Enology, University of Nova Gorica, Vipava, Slovenia Reviewed by: Michaela Griesser, University of Natural Resources        The use of more salt stress-tolerant vine rootstocks can be a sustainable strategy for and Life Sciences, Vienna, Austria R. Andres Zurita-Silva, adapting traditional grapevine cultivars to future conditions. However, how the new M$_1$ Instituto de Investigaciones      and M$_4$ rootstocks perform against salinity compared to conventional ones, such as the Agropecuarias, Chile      1103-Paulsen, had not been previously assessed under real field conditions. Therefore, Federico Berli, Instituto de Biología Agrícola      a field trial was carried out in a young ‘Tempranillo’ (Vitis vinifera L.) vineyard grafted de Mendoza (IBAM),        onto all three rootstocks under a semi-arid and hot-summer Mediterranean climate. The CONICET-UNCuyo, Argentina vines were irrigated with two kinds of water: a non-saline Control with EC of 0.8 dS *Correspondence: Ignacio Buesa m−1 and a Saline treatment with 3.5 dS m−1 . Then, various physiological parameters [EMAIL_REDACTED]          were assessed in the scion, and, additionally, gene expression was studied by high throughput sequencing in leaf and berry tissues. Plant water relations evidenced the Specialty section: This article was submitted to osmotic effect of water quality, but not that of the rootstock. Accordingly, leaf-level gas Plant Abiotic Stress,     exchange rates were also reduced in all three rootstocks, with M$_1$ inducing significantly a section of the journal lower net photosynthesis rates than 1103-Paulsen. Nevertheless, the expression of Frontiers in Plant Science groups of genes involved in photosynthesis and amino acid metabolism pathways were Received: 30 January 2022 Accepted: 25 April 2022           not significantly and differentially expressed. The irrigation with saline water significantly Published: 06 June 2022            increased leaf chloride contents in the scion onto the M-rootstocks, but not onto the Citation:      1103P. The limitation for leaf Cl− and Na+ accumulation on the scion was conferred Buesa I, Pérez-Pérez JG, Visconti F, Strah R, Intrigliolo DS, by rootstock. Few processes were differentially regulated in the scion in response to Bonet L, Gruden K, Pompe-Novak M             the saline treatment, mainly, in the groups of genes involved in the flavonoids and and de Paz JM (2022) Physiological phenylpropanoids metabolic pathways. However, these transcriptomic effects were not and Transcriptional Responses to Saline Irrigation of Young      fully reflected in grape phenolic ripeness, with M$_4$ being the only one that did not cause ‘Tempranillo’ Vines Grafted Onto         reductions in these compounds in response to salinity, and 1103-Paulsen having the Different Rootstocks. Front. Plant Sci. 13:866053. highest overall concentrations. These results suggest that all three rootstocks confer doi: 10.3389/fpls.2022.866053           short-term salinity tolerance to the scion. FIGURE 3 | A subset of significantly enriched gene sets obtained by GSEA (The full set is presented in Supplementary Table 2). Values represent the percentages of genes that were positively (+) or negatively (–) regulated within a particular bin in leaves and berries of cv. ‘Tempranillo’ vines grafted onto 1103-Paulsen, M$_1$, or M$_4$ rootstocks subjected to salinity irrigation. Only statistically significant (FDR q value < 0.25) values are shown. Red denotes positive enrichment or upregulation and green denotes negative enrichment or downregulation. C, control; S, salinity; 1P, 1103-Paulsen rootstock; M$_1$, M$_1$ rootstock; M$_4$, M$_4$ rootstock; L, leaves; B, berries. [SOURCE: Page_10]

FIGURE 4 | Log^2 FC values of genes involved in anthocyanin synthesis in Tempranillo in salinity-treated berries as compared to controls grafted onto 1103-Paulsen, M$_1$, and M$_4$. The specific gene names are provided by means of the Vitvi identifiers. Color represents the value of Log^2 FC.
